Subsea Cables Act. A core proposal of the act requires the President to impose sanctions 
on foreign actors who intentionally damage critical undersea infrastructure, including 
subsea fiber optic cables.27 If passed, this act will build upon the US’s 2020 Undersea 
Cable Control Act, which focused more on preventing adversaries from accessing 
technology to build and operate undersea cables. Together, the two bills aim to deny 
countries like Russia and China the ability to conduct what a sponsoring senator referred 
to as “shadow warfare” against US critical infrastructure in subsea cables.28  
Finally, a landmark development to watch is the recent US Federal Communications 
Commission (FCC) vote to extend cable surveillance to terrestrial endpoints, such as 
cable landing stations and the hardware housed within them that converts the subsea 
optical fiber signals. The FCC has identified these endpoints as chokepoints vulnerable to 
data interception, physical sabotage, and cyber espionage. Under the new rules, 
hyperscalers and tech firms employing AI infrastructure could receive expedited licensing 
for their terrestrial hardware, while foreign rivals would need to apply for individual 
licenses and face the possibility of denial. This is a potential win for the hyperscalers, 
although the pool of eligible hardware suppliers may significantly shrink, creating short-
term bottlenecks in cable landing equipment manufacturing.29   
Europe has historically had a strong presence in the subsea cable industry, but this 
position is being challenged by competing US and Chinese interests and the rise of US 
hyperscaler control. France remains a leading global supplier through its ownership of 
Alcatel Submarine Networks and Orange Marine, while Italy’s Elletra holds a strong mid-
tier position.   
The EU is attempting to carry out a coordinated approach towards cable security via its 
2025 EU Action Plan on Cable Security, but implementation has been slow. In June of this 
year, however, the European Commission finalized a €5.8mn grant to establish the first two 
regional cable hubs in the Baltic Sea and the Mediterranean Sea, and a separate €40mn 
fund to increase its submarine cable repair capacity.30  
Finally, US-allied attempts to find a geopolitically secure “Fourth Route” away from volatile 
transits in the Red Sea and South China Sea have led to the creation of the Far North Fiber 
express route. This will be the first long-haul submarine cable connecting Europe and Asia 
exclusively through Allied-sovereign waters. It is expected to cost $1.17bn and should be 
ready for service by the end of 2026.31 The project is owned by US Alaskan company Far 
North Digital, in conjunction with its Canadian affiliate True North Global Networks, but it 
has since expanded into a consortium with companies from Japan and Finland as well. 
Alcatel Submarine Networks will supply the cables.   
All of this strategic interest has translated into unprecedented subsea cable infrastructure 
investment, estimated to have averaged over $2bn per year in new construction over the 
last 9 years.32 Most new investment is forecast to be directed toward trans-pacific routes.    
The most consequential response, however, is coming from the private sector, which 
controls most of the infrastructure. AI is sharply increasing bandwidth demand and 
prompting content and cloud providers—notably Google through its Pacific Connect

3. Satellites: Beyond terrestrial networks  
Like subsea cables, satellites form a part of the Internet’s physical layer. Throughout much 
of the 20th century, satellites carried the bulk of all transoceanic digital connectivity. The 
launch of Telstar, the first operational telecommunications satellite in 1962, was soon 
followed by other satellites that helped commercialize the international communications 
and broadcast industry.  
Today, however, traditional satellite communications (satcom) complement rather than 
replace cables. Satellites provide broadcast and communication services to sparsely 
populated regions not served by terrestrial infrastructure and act as a strategic back-up for 
disaster-prone regions. While subsea cables deliver more consistent, high-capacity links 
between major population centres, satellites are a cost-effective solution for governments 
seeking to connect regions with low population densities.34  
The renewed focus on satellite communications is driven less by their current share of 
global traffic than by their strategic potential. Two forces are decisive. First, the war in 
Ukraine and US-China technology competition are prompting sovereign investment in 
space-based communications. Second, rising AI workloads are intensifying demand for 
bandwidth and encouraging the deployment of satellite mega-constellations. We examine 
the implications in greater detail in the next chapter. 
For these reasons, communications-based satellite networks and space-based 
connectivity represent the next major frontier in the contest for control over digital 
connectivity.   
3.1 How satellites support connectivity  
There are three main types of satellites in operation: communications, Earth-observation, 
and radiofrequency- based. This section focuses on communication satellites, which 
provide services ranging from radio, TV , telecommunications, and Internet by receiving 
signals from Earth and retransmitting them to a ground station elsewhere, creating a 
communications channel between distant regions that were previously unable to 
communicate with one another.

33 The State of the Network: 2026 Edition. 2026. Telegeography. 
34 Carter L., Burnett D., Drew S., Marle G., Hagadorn L., Bartlett-McNeil D., and Irvine N. (2009). Submarine Cables and the 
Oceans – Connecting the World. UNEP-WCMC Biodiversity Series No. 31. ICPC/UNEP/UNEP-WCMC.

### Page 11

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
The Great Rebalancing (4/5): The connectivity chokepoint – September 2, 2026

11

Traditional satellite communications (also referred to as “satcom”) rely on large 
geostationary (GEO) satellites positioned about 35,800 kilometres above Earth. Systems 
such as the early Intelsat 1 (1965) and the more recent Jupiter 3 (2023), provide broad, 
fixed coverage with high availability and favourable ground-terminal geometry.35 GEO 
assets have historically supported predictable business models because of their long 
operating lives, typically around 15 years, and high launch costs. Their strategic value is 
clear, but so is their limitation: distance creates latency. The farther a satellite is from 
Earth, the longer data takes to travel, which is one reason subsea cables continue to carry 
most global connectivity. 
In addition, GEO satellites also suffer from increasingly longer development cycles and 
higher integration complexity. As a result, both state and private actors are turning their 
interest towards low-earth orbit (LEO) satellites instead. LEO satellites orbit between 160 
and 1,500km above the Earth’s surface, enabling a more seamless user experience for 
applications like live streaming, video calls, remote desktops, etc. Because they circle the 
planet up to 16 times a day, LEO constellations can provide ubiquitous coverage to nearly 
any point on Earth. SpaceX’s Starlink was the harbinger of the LEO satellite model, but 
several LEO constellations have emerged in response to Starlink since then, including 
Amazon LEO, OneWeb (now part of Eutelsat), Telesat Lightspeed, and China’s Guowang 
and Qianfan projects. In fact, in July 2026 Amazon announced it had filed an application 
with the US FCC to deploy a constellation of more than 5,100 satellites that would provide 
direct-to-device connectivity by 2028.36 Doing so will directly challenge Starlink Mobile, a 
subset of Starlink comprised of around 650 satellites that beam mobile data to phones in 
remote regions on earth, and which has recently also announced plans to launch 
“thousands” more satellites by end of 2028.37  
3.2 Why satellites are strategic: the arrival Starlink  
Although subsea cables carry the bulk of internet traffic, communications-based satellites 
are also integral to the global economy. They provide connectivity to remote and 
underserved regions that undersea cables cannot reach, while also enabling 
communications for mobile platforms such as ships, aircraft and military assets. Indeed, 
industries such as aviation, rail, shipping and logistics depend on satellites for navigation, 
route optimization, and cargo tracking.   
Perhaps to an even greater extent than cables, satcom has become strategic to a country’s 
military infrastructure. Satcom companies are developing partnerships to cater to civilian 
and government forces. For example, Canada’s Telesat (c. 1969) announced a strategic 
partnership with the Canadian Armed Forces to develop and deliver a multifrequency, 
Arctic military satellite communications capability via its forthcoming Lightspeed 
constellation, which the company targets to launch in late 2026.38 Meanwhile, established 
satcom companies with significant government -related revenue streams, like Viaset, SES 
and Eutelsat, have seen strong growth over the past year.  Our DB equity analysts estimate 
that existing GEO communications contracts alone account for around $50bn-60bn in 
defence-related spending (excluding non-US allies).39  
The greatest harbinger has been SpaceX’s Starlink, which has provided encrypted 
communications, imagery and geolocation services for the Ukrainian military against 
Russia since 2022. In addition, Starlink’s “direct-to-device” business model that connects 
smartphones directly to satellites threatens to collapse the traditional, hierarchical

Internet infrastructure, where cables traverse terrestrial data and comms-based satellites 
take it on its last leg.  
Starlink’s ability to bypass the conventional hierarchy of connectivity provided vital 
communications for Ukrainian forces and civilians at a time when conventional 
communications like fiber lines, cellular towers and power grids were damaged early in 
the war. This highlighted how a private company could become essential to a nation’s 
military capability during a war. The gravity of this power was demonstrated again in 
February 2026, when SpaceX and Ukraine introduced a terminal verification system that 
effectively blocked Russia from using Starlink military terminals, after it was revealed that 
Russia had acquired Starlink terminals via third-party countries and grey markets to 
conduct strike drones.   
The entry of Starlink in 2019 has shifted the goalposts and urgency for more 
communications-based satellites. Bringing affordable, high-speed internet to those that 
lack access to traditional broadband, Starlink’s true purpose is to help deploy Musk’s goal 
of AI compute at a massive scale. In fact, Musk confirmed that Starlink is also designed to 
support a project called Starmind, which aims to place massive data centers in space. He 
recently filed applications with the FCC for a constellation of space-based AI data center 
satellites through this project. These space AI data centers will compute in space but then 
use lasers to offload the processed data directly into the Starlink constellation, which will 
beam the results back to Earth with low latency.  
By using Starlink to create a reliable and robust orbital network used for Earth, Musk hopes 
to eventually extend the satellite internet service to the Moon, Mars, and beyond. Our 
equity analysts place Musk’s aims for orbital data centers within a 5-year horizon and 
human voyage and colonization in Mars and off-world within 10-year horizon.  
Starlink has upped the game for the rest of the world considerably. First, by its sheer 
dominance in the number of satellites it operates and in the numbers of subscribers it now 
has on its network. Starlink now accounts for 54% of all operational satellites in orbit,40 
and our equity analysts estimate Starlink’s consumer broadband subscribers will grow to 
around 17mn by the end of 2026.41 Second, its ability to simultaneously increase speeds 
while cutting hardware prices has made it very hard for anyone else to compete. Finally, 
Starlink’s entry into LEO also made it harder for pure-play GEO industry players like Viaset 
and Intelsat to focus solely on consumer broadband, although as mentioned above there 
is a growing market opportunity for these players to enter the government services market. 
We will go more in depth on Starlink and its parent company SpaceX in our final chapter on 
Cloud and Data Centers.

3.3 Satellite vulnerabilities 
Because communications-based satellites are so critical, their vulnerability to deliberate 
attacks is a matter of strategic concern. Satellites are susceptible to attacks such as 
jamming (when the uplink or downlink frequency is filled with noise so the signal cannot 
get through) and spoofing (transmitting fake signals) of their radio waves. Countries like 
Finland, Estonia and Poland have all reported GNSS disruptions associated with alleged 
Russian activities in the Baltic region, which has affected numerous services such as 
commercial aircraft and maritime navigation. Other examples of disruption include 
cyberattacks on satellites via their ground stations and control networks, as well as 
physical and cyberattacks on the orbiting satellites themselves. Notable incidents include 
evidence that Russia was conducting widespread jamming of Starlink GPS signals soon 
after their invasion of Ukraine in 2022. In 2021, Russia also conducted an anti-satellite 
weapon test that destroyed an inactive satellite and generated a massive field of orbital 
debris.

3.4 How state actors are responding   
A rapid, high-stakes military space race looks to be underway between the US, China, and 
Russia, focused on establishing, securing, and defending satellite communication 
(satcom) constellations, particularly those in Low Earth Orbit (LEO).  
While US companies currently dominate the satcom landscape, they face a growing 
challenge from other countries, especially China. China is developing its own satcom

The Great Rebalancing (4/5): The connectivity chokepoint – September 2, 2026

14

ecosystem through two key initiatives: the state-run Guowang network, aimed towards 
China’s domestic telecom and national security, and the Shanghai-backed Qianfen 
constellation, which is aimed at the commercial and export markets. Together, these 
projects aim to deploy between 13,000 and 15,000 satellites.42 Guowang is designed 
primarily to connect rural areas and government institutions, while Qianfan is more 
commercially focused, aiming to provide in-flight connectivity (IFC) and direct-to-device 
(D2D) communications, with active pilots underway in Brazil, Malaysia, and Thailand.   
Both constellations are in their early stages, with fewer than 200 satellites each currently 
in orbit. In December 2025, China also submitted filings with the International 
Telecommunication Union (ITU) to reserve orbital slots and spectrum for nearly 200,000 
additional satellites – a move to secure future resources, even though China does not yet 
have the launch capacity to deploy such a number.43 To facilitate more launch activity, 
China has also implemented new regulatory guidelines to facilitate IPOs for firms 
developing reusable rockets.  
The EU is also attempting to reduce its dependence on US private operators by building 
IRIS, a government-controlled constellation of approximately 290 satellites, with services 
targeted to begin in 2029. However, progress on the project remains slow. Other notable 
sovereign interests include the UK’s stake in OneWeb and Canada’s investment in 
Telesat’s Lightspeed project.  
Driven by these strategic imperatives, the market for communications-based satellites is 
growing, as they become critical infrastructure for consumers, enterprises, and 
governments. Deutsche Bank equity analysts estimate the satcom market grew by over 
35% YoY to nearly $26bn in 2025, mostly driven by consumer broadband and government 
contracts.44 This growth is expected to continue, with market revenue projected to reach 
$65-70bn by 2030.

connectivity stocks

4. The new power map of connectivity  
4.1 The rise of private control  
While cloud and space data centers will be the focus of our next chapter, this section 
zooms in how connectivity supply chains are being affected by the AI build-out.  
In the connectivity model set out in Section 1, hyperscalers own the destination of data: 
the cloud, where applications, websites and AI models run. Historically, these companies 
have relied on telecom operators for transmission and concentrated investment on data 
centers and cloud services. That division is now eroding, as hyperscalers are increasingly 
acquiring, funding or building the underlying infrastructure, including subsea cables and 
communications satellites—that carry data to and from their platforms. 
AI’s data intensity is accelerating this vertical integration. In 2016, traditional backbone 
providers carried most international bandwidth; today, content and cloud networks 
account for nearly three-quarters of demand.45 Owning connectivity allows hyperscalers 
to lower long-run unit costs, scale capacity with cloud demand and reduce dependence 
on third-party networks. It also gives them greater control over the economics and 
governance of the data chain. 
This trend is evident in new cable construction, where private companies are starting to 
swallow up market share. For example, of the $14bn projected investment in new 
submarine cables entering service between 2025 and 2027, driven largely by content 
providers such as Google and Meta rather than the traditional internet backbone 
providers.46 While traditional telecom operators still account for the majority of existing 
builds, this new investment demonstrates the steady and growing influence of large-scale 
cloud and content companies in shaping global connectivity.  
The same dynamic is beginning to emerge in the satellite ecosystem, where hyperscalers 
and space technology companies are exploring space-based data centers as a potential 
long-term solution to the terrestrial constraints that increasingly accompany the buildout 
of AI compute capacity.  
To make orbital computing viable, companies need communications infrastructure 
capable of moving massive amounts of data between space and Earth, creating a strategic 
rationale for greater ownership of satellite networks and related connectivity assets. A 
notable entrant is Jeff Bezos’ Blue Origin, which announced TeraWave in January 2026, a 
multi-orbit communications network comprising 5,280 satellites in LEO and 128 satellites 
in MEO. Blue Origin plans to begin deploying the constellation in 4Q27 and is targeting 
enterprise, data-center, and government customers. DB analysts estimate that the 
constellation could require ~$18bn of capex.  
Although Blue Origin has not explicitly positioned TeraWave as infrastructure for orbital 
data centers, its high-capacity, optically interconnected network could eventually provide 
part of the communications layer required to connect space-based compute systems with 
terrestrial networks. In addition to SpaceX and Blue Origin, Amazon is building its LEO 
communications constellation, while Nvidia-backed Starcloud has filed for regulatory 
approval to deploy orbital data-center satellites. Starcloud also plans to launch AWS 
Outposts hardware aboard its second satellite in October 2026, providing an early test of 
AWS infrastructure operating in space.

45 The State of the Network: 2026 Edition. 2026. Telegeography. 
46 Ibid.

4.2 Effects of the AI disruption 
The result is a sharp shift in control over global connectivity. Traditional carriers are 
increasingly becoming minority consortium members or implementation partners, while 
hyperscalers lead funding and technical design. The Internet’s decentralised architecture 
increasingly coexists with concentrated private ownership of critical connectivity 
infrastructure. 
The consequences are substantial. By owning subsea cables and satellite networks, 
hyperscalers are increasingly able to build private connectivity corridors linking their data 
centres, reducing dependence on traditional telecom operators. This vertical integration 
bypasses—and may ultimately compress—the tiered, multi-provider structure that has 
defined telecommunications for decades. 
This disruption has forced traditional telecom operators to either merge to survive or 
retreat into more niche services. By shrinking the telecommunications market, the 
remaining players can better afford the massive capex required for increasing broadband 
demand. Notable deals in recent years include $7.3bn merger between US-based Viasat 
and British satellite firm Inmarsat in 2023, SES’s acquisition of Intelsat for $3.1bn in 2024, 
and the $8bn Rocket Lab/Iridium47 consolidation in 2026. By creating these multi-orbit 
groups, they have been able to concentrate on key segments of mass-market 
connectivity.  
Europe is perhaps at the greatest disadvantage in this new connectivity map, finding itself 
crowded out by US-led private innovators and state-backed Chinese companies. The 
European Centre for Development Policy Management argues that the growing 
concentration of subsea-cable ownership and operational control among US hyperscalers 
is creating structural dependencies that could erode Europe’s digital sovereignty. 48 This 
concern is particularly acute for Europe, where ownership and operational control of 
critical subsea infrastructure is increasingly concentrated among non-EU actors. Unlike 
the United States, which exercises oversight through institutions such as the FCC, Europe 
lacks a unified governance framework for the subsea cable ecosystem, raising concerns 
about long-term digital sovereignty.49  
The same risk exists in space, where Starlink dominates the global satellite network. As 
the CEO of the French telecoms operator, Bouygues Telecom, warned in an interview 
earlier this year, Europe needs to “get some sovereignty” or else it risks having its

47 Deutsche Bank and/or its affiliates is acting as advisor to Rocket Lab Corporation on the potential acquisition of Iridium 
Communications, Inc.. 
48 Pearson, Sasha. n.d. “Troubled Waters: Europe’s Subsea Telecommunications Network. ” Ecdpm. 
https://ecdpm.org/work/troubled-waters-europes-subsea-telecommunications-network. 
49 Ibid.

### Page 17

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
The Great Rebalancing (4/5): The connectivity chokepoint – September 2, 2026

17

connectivity controlled by a non-state actor like Starlink.50 Reflecting these pressures, in 
June 2026, Bouygues itself partnered with Orange and Free-illiad Group to acquire and 
break up French telecommunications company SFR for €20.35bn.   
In the space arena, the EU has attempted to address this problem with its proposed Space 
Act, which aims to cultivate European champions. However, this has created friction with 
the US, whose FCC has publicly warned that Washington would retaliate if Europe 
imposed protectionist measures that favored its own satellite companies over American 
ones.51 In the meantime, the formidable presence of Starlink continues to drive a new 
wave of private communications-based satellite initiatives seeking to compete.52    
States therefore face a strategic dilemma. AI and cloud adoption are directing more traffic 
through hyperscaler-owned networks, increasing dependence on private technology 
companies even as governments must guarantee resilience and control in a crisis. The US 
is best positioned because its firms dominate the industry. European governments have 
responded by buying stakes in, or taking control of, strategic telecom assets, including 
Italy’s acquisition of TIM Sparkle and Spain’s 10% stake in Telefónica.

Figure 8: Aside from 2022, where activity dropped sharply amidst the backdrop of 
Russia Ukraine, the telecoms M&A industry has seen massive growth

announcement data. Data up to July 2026.

5. Conclusion 
Digital connectivity emerged from an ideal of neutrality and disintermediation. The Internet 
was designed to share knowledge across research institutions, while cables and 
communications satellites expanded international exchange. Today, connectivity is an 
arena of state and corporate power. Control over data flows is becoming as consequential 
as control over payment infrastructure, and the race for AI leadership is intensifying the 
strategic value of the networks that carry information. 
Subsea cables are uniquely fragile because their criticality is matched by limited 
substitutability. The parallel with payments is instructive: excluding a country from SWIFT 
can amount to financial siege; severing its cable links can impose a digital one. Yet 
payments have emerging alternatives—including CIPS, SPFS and stablecoins—whereas 
subsea cables have few practical substitutes. A replacement cable cannot be laid during a 
crisis, so a major disruption could simultaneously impair payments, markets, military 
command-and-control and basic communications.

18

The most important actor to watch may not be a state but a company: SpaceX. Starlink 
has reshaped satellite communications on cost, scale and performance, compressing the 
traditional hierarchy of space connectivity. Its ambitions now extend into markets 
historically served by terrestrial telecom and cable providers, in both advanced and 
emerging economies.53 The implications for governments, defence and incumbent 
telecom operators will be significant. 
In the era of infrastructure realism, power lies less in the number of cables or satellites 
built than in control of the network’s junction points and architecture—from the ocean 
floor to low-Earth orbit. As with dollar-based payments and Taiwan’s semiconductor 
capacity, concentration in cable landing rights and orbital slots creates a new class of 
systemic risk. The central question is no longer who wins the cable race, but who controls 
the rails through which global value flows—and on whose terms. 
The final part of our series turns to data centres, moving the chokepoint further up the 
technology stack—from minerals and cables at the physical layer, through chips at the 
fabrication layer, to the infrastructure that stores and processes data at scale. 
We would like to thank Edison Yu, Laura Li, Benjamin Black, Raymond Wong and Yi Xiong 
for their valuable insights and comments to this paper.

Document 363: The revenge of the supply-side_ A new economic regime (2026-09-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The revenge of the supply-side_ A new economic regime (2026-09-14).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

The revenge of the supply-side:  
A new economic regime

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Strategist

September 14, 2026 
Back in the 2010s, macroeconomic policy was preoccupied by insufficient 
demand. In historical terms, it was an unusual period of low inflation and low 
interest rates, where supply-side issues were considered peripheral. If anything, 
it went almost unquestioned that the supply side of the economy would scale up 
to meet higher demand. 
But in the 2020s so far, we’re back in a supply-driven world. Growth has been 
constrained as much by supply shortages as deficient demand. This year alone, 
we’ve seen major consequences from the closure of the Strait of Hormuz, with 
ramifications for oil prices, inflation and economic growth. 
This macro regime shift, where supply rather than demand is the bigger issue, is 
a tricky environment to navigate, and renders much of the 2010s playbook 
redundant: 
— First, inflation spikes will occur more frequently in a supply-constrained 
world, as there isn’t the spare economic capacity to meet higher 
demand. 
— Second, monetary stimulus also becomes less effective in this 
environment, as traditional demand-side monetary tools can’t deal with 
the direct cause of supply shocks. 
— Third, fiscal stimulus in a supply-constrained environment becomes 
much more inflationary and is more likely to crowd out private 
investment. 
Looking forward, the return to a supply-driven world means that the primary 
issue for policymakers will be expanding the productive frontier of the economy. 
After years where insufficient demand was the main preoccupation, the future of 
growth now rests on a supply-side expansion that can raise living standards 
across the board.

### Page 2

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
The paradigm shift in thinking: How the biggest economic
problem moved from demand to supply
The legacy of the Global Financial Crisis led to a world of demand deficiency
In 2008, the Global Financial Crisis (GFC) marked the worst economic contraction
since the Great Depression of the 1930s. Unemployment rose to multi-decade
highs and the subsequent recovery was very sluggish, despite huge economic

stimulus on a scale previously unheard of in peacetime. This slow recovery was
clear in numerous ways, but an obvious one was that the US unemployment rate
didn’t return to its pre-GFC low until 2017, a full decade later. Meanwhile in the
Eurozone, it didn’t happen until February 2020, just as the pandemic then sent it
higher once again.
This was a world where deficient demand was the main preoccupation of
macroeconomic policymaking. Indeed, the Depression-era concept of “secular

stagnation” made a comeback, in which the equilibrium interest rate consistent
with full employment was negative. In this world, the constraints of the zero lower
bound on monetary policy would make it very difficult to get to full employment.
This demand deficiency manifested itself in persistent inflation undershoots.
Throughout the 2010s, both the Federal Reserve and the ECB missed their 2%
inflation targets on the downside. Policy rates remained close to the zero lower

bound, and long-term market interest rates also moved continuously lower. Even
before the pandemic, Germany’s 10-year bund yield fell to -0.7% in summer 2019.
In this environment of negative yields, borrowing was basically a “free lunch”.
At the time, there were genuine fears about a permanent low-growth trap. Indeed,
when the Covid-19 pandemic began and another economic contraction was
underway, there were initially concerns that deflationary pressures would intensify

further. For instance, the 10-year US inflation swap (a market-based measure of
expectations for US CPI over the next decade) fell to just 0.81% in March 2020.
Investors genuinely thought that low inflation and low interest rates were likely to
persist for the decade ahead as well, and the 10-year Treasury yield closed at a
record low of 0.51% in August 2020.
This fear of deficient demand informed the policy response to Covid-19. The
stimulus packages went well beyond those seen in the GFC. For instance, no time

was lost in slashing rates back to the zero lower bound, and the fiscal deficits went
far above anything previously seen in peacetime. In fact, the US federal deficit stood
at -14.5% of GDP in 2020, the biggest since 1945. And with much of the global
economy locked down for months, consumers accumulated huge “excess
savings” as they couldn’t spend on their normal activities.
The pandemic was the turning point towards a world of supply constraints

With hindsight, we can see that all this stimulus and the accumulation of excess
savings created a huge demand surge, just as the global economy was
experiencing major supply-chain issues as it reopened:
n The lockdown periods saw a big rotation in spending away from services
(like dining out, travel, gym memberships) towards durable goods (like
home office equipment, electronics, exercise equipment). This
overwhelmed existing supply chains.
n Pandemic-related disruption led to major issues with shipping. A severe

### Page 3

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
shortage of containers led freight rates to rise more than 5-fold at their peak.
n Labour markets were disrupted, as many took early retirement during the
pandemic or changed occupations. Companies therefore had to raise
wages to compete for a smaller pool of workers.
n Commodity prices also surged in this period, particularly energy. The
pandemic period saw producers cut back on drilling and exploration. But

this meant it was more difficult to dial up supply when demand started to
rise again, and energy prices surged back, with Brent crude oil moving
above its pre-pandemic levels in mid-to-late 2021.
These supply constraints marked a major shift from the environment of the 2010s.
Back then, it was almost an unquestioned assumption that the supply-side of the
economy would scale up to meet demand. Inflation had been low and the most
obvious signs of supply-side stress were not apparent. But now the reverse was

true, and inflation was gathering pace again.
Initially, many argued that this burst of inflation would prove temporary. They
argued it was down to one-off factors like the pandemic and the subsequent
reopening. Moreover, there was a view that the longer-term disinflationary forces
of the previous 30 years would still reassert themselves. But by late-2021, it was
clear that the combination of huge stimulus, the drawdown of excess savings, and
serious supply-side disruption had created more inflation than anticipated. By

December 2021, US CPI had risen to its highest level since 1982, at 7.0%, whilst
Eurozone CPI was at its highest since 1991, at 5.0%.

The transition to a supply-constrained world accelerated dramatically in early 2022
with Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. Energy prices rose significantly, and European
industry was forced to decouple from Russian gas. Meanwhile, the blockade of
Ukraine’s Black Sea ports restricted the supply of agricultural commodities like

wheat and corn, driving food prices higher still.
This sent inflation even higher, up to multi-decade highs around the world. US CPI
inflation peaked at 9.1% in June 2022, and Eurozone CPI peaked at 10.6% in
October 2022. This was the context in which central banks launched into the most
aggressive rate hikes in a generation. Over 2022-23, the Federal Reserve hiked rates
by 525bps, and the ECB delivered 450bps.

### Page 4

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
These supply issues persisted long after the pandemic-related issues were over
Yet even as the worst of that inflation wave passed, the 2023-24 period kept
demonstrating how supply-side disruptions could lead to wider economic
problems. For instance, attacks from the Houthi rebels in the Red Sea forced major
shipping lines to reroute away from the Suez Canal around the Cape of Good Hope,

adding over a week to the length of trips. Meanwhile, an El Nino-induced drought
meant transit capacity through the Panama Canal fell 30%, forcing ships to wait in
long queues or reroute instead. And in Europe, repeated summer heatwaves have
lowered the Rhine’s water levels, disrupting industrial barges and affecting
industries like chemicals and steel.

More broadly, the other shift in the post-pandemic period was the growing use of
strategic industrial policy, particularly as governments sought to boost domestic
manufacturing capacity. For instance, the US passed the Inflation Reduction Act

under the Biden administration, which encouraged clean energy investments, as
well as the CHIPS Act, which sought to boost semiconductor manufacturing. Tariffs
also became a more enduring feature of the landscape, and the Biden
administration hiked tariffs on Chinese imports, with those on EVs up from 25% to
100%, whilst semiconductors rose from 25% to 50%.
Then under the second Trump administration, tariffs became an even bigger part of
the policy toolkit, with the US effective tariff rate surging from 2.6% beforehand to

a peak of 23% in April 2025, before settling around 11% currently. Several different
measures were introduced, but the macro impacts were clear. In particular, there
was a major wave of front-loading of imports in Q1 2025, as those trading
internationally sought to get ahead of the announcements. Indeed, the surge in
imports meant US GDP contracted slightly in Q1 2025. Moreover, other countries
moved to respond, and not just with tariffs of their own. For instance, China

introduced export controls on rare earths.
Whilst 2025 was dominated by tariffs, 2026 then brought a fresh supply shock for
the global economy with the closure of the Strait of Hormuz. Previously, around
25% of the world’s seaborne oil trade had gone through the Strait, including nearly
15 million barrels per day of crude oil. But the start of the US-Iran conflict led the
Strait of Hormuz to close, and six months later only limited traffic is still able to pass

through. This led to another supply shock, with Brent crude oil prices moving back

Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
above $100/bbl, whilst European natural gas futures also reached their highest
levels since late-2022. Yet again, the biggest global economic disruption of the year
to date has been a supply shock, rather than a demand one.

Types of supply shocks
Broadly speaking, we can distinguish between three sorts of supply shocks,
although there can be considerable overlap between these categories.
1. Temporary supply shocks that have a short-term economic impact.
Example: low river levels blocking a key trade route.

The first category of supply shocks are those that have a temporary impact on the
economy. Examples include the blockage of the Suez Canal in 2021 when the Ever
Given container ship ran aground, or a drought that causes river levels to fall and
means fewer ships can get through key waterways.
These are supply shocks that temporarily remove capacity, but then see a bounce
back to “normal”, broadly speaking. For instance, a drought is hopefully not

repeated, and when river levels return to normal, supply can resume again.
For the economy, this means the longer-term consequences are also limited. The
inflation impact is mean-reverting, and output recovers, often in a V-shape. These
are the sort of shocks that central banks are usually able to “look through”, because
they don’t cause permanent structural damage to the economy.
2.Medium-term supply shocks that can last several years, with a clear economic

impact.
Example: the closure of the Strait of Hormuz.
These kinds of shocks are often driven by geopolitical events or policy decisions,
and are longer-lasting shocks which do not have a quick solution. A good example
is the current closure of the Strait of Hormuz, but we have seen many in recent years.
In theory, these shocks can end, but their length is uncertain and they have material
economic impacts. An increase in tariffs also fits into this category, as it forces an

adjustment in trade flows, even if they can be reversed in theory.

### Page 6

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
A good example of this kind of supply shock is the 1973 oil shock. Back in 1973, the
oil embargo by OAPEC led oil prices to nearly quadruple, and they remained at that
higher level for years after. It wasn’t simply a temporary adjustment that normalised
again.
Unlike the short-term shocks, these medium-term shocks can force a permanent
rerouting of trade flows and supply chains. Moreover, they can push inflation higher

for an extended period and are more likely to filter through into core inflation
categories. And because they are not temporary shocks that quickly adjust,
policymakers can’t “look through” them as easily. As such, central banks aren’t
able to accommodate the shock, and often have to hike rates to clamp down on the
resulting inflation, as we saw in 2022.
3.Structural supply trends that affect the longer-term growth rate.
Example: Demographic trends.

This category includes the long-term forces on the supply side that affect the
economy’s growth rate. A good example are demographics and the current
situation of ageing populations. Another example are the multi-decade processes
of globalization and deglobalization.
These are factors that can permanently shift the productive frontier of the economy.
For example, the current situation of ageing populations means that a smaller
proportion of the population are of working-age, meaning that labour forces are

shrinking. As a result, that constrains the growth rate of potential GDP, meaning
that more growth must come from higher productivity rather than a larger number
of workers.
These shifting trends can lead to permanent changes in so-called r*, which is the
equilibrium interest rate where full employment is achieved – a level that neither
stimulates nor restricts economic activity. In other words, these factors are capable
of shifting the economy’s potential growth rate, at which it can grow sustainably

without generating inflationary pressures.
What happened the last time the supply-side dominated:
Lessons from the 1970s
As we consider the return of the supply side, it’s worth bearing in mind this is far
from the first time that supply shocks have dominated the landscape. Back in the
1970s, the biggest economic shocks of that decade were also supply driven.
That decade saw two huge oil shocks in 1973 and 1979. In the first, the Organization
of Arab Petroleum Exporting Countries (OAPEC) placed an embargo on several

countries, including the United States, in retaliation for their support for Israel in the
Yom Kippur War. This caused oil prices to nearly quadruple, triggering a recession
across multiple countries that began in late 1973. Then in 1979, a second oil shock
occurred around the time of the Iranian Revolution. That coincided with a big
decline in Iran’s oil output, before the Iran-Iraq War in 1980 led to further production
declines for both countries. As a result, oil prices more than doubled again.

To be fair, there were other demand-side and supply-side shocks that meant
inflation got out of hand in the 1970s. But the two oil shocks were the single biggest
factors, and created a stagflationary environment in which inflation accelerated

### Page 7

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
and growth slowed.
Figure 7: WTI Oil Prices ($/bbl) in real terms

This experience is instructive for today, as the 1970s demonstrated how a repeated
set of supply shocks could act in a cumulative way. After all, even if the textbook

response is to “look through” a temporary supply shock, a series of consecutive
shocks can suddenly feel very permanent when they happen one-after-another.
That continuous disruption can then cause inflation expectations to become
unanchored, risking the kind of dynamics that trigger wage-price spirals. Indeed,
much as economists can focus on “core inflation” that excludes food and energy
prices, these are essential goods that are difficult to substitute away from, and play

a key role in inflation expectations.
On the bright side, the more optimistic lesson is that supply shocks often force
adjustments that prevent a repeat. For instance, back in the 1970s, there was a
recognition that Western economies had become overly dependent on Middle
Eastern oil, and major efforts were made to reduce that dependence. For instance,
the US established the Strategic Petroleum Reserve ( SPR) in 1975, and the
Department of Energy was established in 1977. Moreover, investments were

directed to US energy production, and since 2019, the US has been a net exporter
of energy, rather than a net importer.
Clearly the 1970s are far from an exact parallel. Most obviously, economies are far
less energy intensive today, so oil shocks don’t have as big an impact today.
Nevertheless, the dynamic of repeated supply shocks is one that bears relevance
given how that dynamic is re-echoing today, and with inflation still lingering above
target in many of the world’s biggest economies.

What are the implications for today?
1. Inflation spikes will occur more frequently, as there isn’t the spare economic

### Page 8

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
capacity to meet higher demand.
The first consequence of a supply-constrained world is that unlike much of the
2010s, there isn’t the spare economic capacity (or a negative output gap) that can
easily absorb higher demand. So if there is an unexpected demand shock, for
whatever reason, the consequence will likely be higher inflation rather than higher
output.
Figure 8: IMF: Output Gap in the G7 economies (% of potential GDP)

-5
-4
-3
-2
-1
0
1
2
3
1980 1990 2000 2010 2020
The 2010s environment of
deficient demand has gone
Source : Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank
This is a particular risk right now because of those longer-term structural shifts on
the supply side. First, global demographics and the retirement of the baby boomers
mean that populations are ageing, and many countries now even have shrinking
working-age populations. This is a big difference from the world of decades past,

when the labour force was generally expanding. Second, the push to reshore
production of key industries has also raised costs, as it requires the duplication of
supply chains. And third, other structural shifts like the push for higher defence
spending have also created an increase in the demand for capital. So there isn't the
spare capacity in the economy to absorb higher demand that there was in the
2010s.
This world of more frequent supply shocks means that central banks must also

trade off how quickly they want to bring inflation back to target against the potential
output volatility that might result. Indeed, we saw this year when the Strait of
Hormuz was blocked, central banks didn’t immediately react with rate hikes, but
they waited a few months to see how the consequences developed. But of course,
that has also meant inflation has stayed above target in many countries, often for
several years. In fact, US PCE inflation has been above 2% since March 2021.

2. Monetary stimulus becomes less effective, as it can’t deal with the direct cause
of supply shocks.
Monetary policy has a complex transmission channel, but when central banks cut
rates, the aim is usually to stimulate demand. This is a very useful tool in demand-
driven recessions like 2008, but it becomes more problematic in the face of supply
shocks.
The problem is that when a supply shock hits, whether that’s a natural disaster, or
the blockage of a key trade route, the economy’s productive capacity drops.

### Page 9

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
Monetary policy cannot affect that directly.
This matters because negative supply shocks have the unfortunate effect of raising
inflation and lowering growth simultaneously. So it puts central banks in a very
tricky position, as they found in the inflationary environment of the 1970s after the
oil shocks. With hindsight, many central banks were too dovish after the first oil
shock and inflation got out of control. In the case of the Federal Reserve, that then

led to a far more hawkish response to the second oil shock, when Paul Volcker
tightened monetary policy significantly to get inflation under control again.
These problems are further exacerbated because central banks operate with
uncertainty about the future. Just as we don’t know today when the Strait of
Hormuz might return to normal, central bankers in 1973 were unsure how long the
oil embargo would last. But because central banks make policy that responds with

a lag, there is inherently the risk of overshooting. If they expect a supply shock to be
permanent, yet it proves temporary, then they risk overtightening. And similarly in
reverse, if disruption is expected to be temporary, but proves permanent, they risk
not doing enough and letting inflation and inflation expectations rise too much.
3. Fiscal stimulus becomes much more likely to cause overheating and crowding
out.
In a supply-constrained world where the economy lacks spare capacity, fiscal

stimulus is more likely to overheat the economy and generate inflation.
This is a big shift from the post-GFC world. Back then, unemployment was high and
deficient demand meant additional government spending didn’t face the same
competition from private investment, so the multipliers from that stimulus were
much higher. Moreover, several countries were able to borrow at incredibly low
interest rates, even negative in some cases.
But this shifted with the pandemic. Not only was the stimulus in 2020 far bigger, but

unlike 2008, the economy then saw a huge wave of pent-up demand once the
pandemic restrictions were lifted, supported by the excess savings accumulated
during the lockdowns. In turn, that contributed to a huge wave of inflation, even
before we then had the additional energy and food price shocks in 2022.
Meanwhile, government bond yields have also returned to their pre-GFC levels,
making the cost of any stimulus far more expensive. And that comes as we are also

seeing a kind of "reverse crowding out", thanks to borrowing from the AI
hyperscalers that is adding to pressure on rates. So in this environment, any fiscal
stimulus will come at a higher cost.
Conclusion: Looking forward
The transition to a supply-driven world means that many of the old economic
assumptions from the 2010s are no longer valid. There’s been a structural break
that means classic demand-side stimulus is not as effective. After years when it was

almost unquestioned that the supply side of the economy would scale up to meet
higher demand, we are now back in a world where supply is a meaningful constraint
again.
As such, the emphasis will naturally shift towards expanding the supply-side of the

### Page 10

14 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 10 Deutsche Bank AG
economy, something we’re already beginning to see. This could be encouraging
more energy production, more housing, or bringing new participants into the
labour force. In essence, what’s needed are policies that raise the productive
potential of the economy, and boost the rate at which the economy can grow
without generating inflationary pressures.
These supply shocks in recent years have been a challenge. Even if they might be

temporary in isolation, the cumulative effect has been to keep inflation elevated,
and we saw that happen back in the 1970s too. This is particularly problematic if the
starting point is above-target inflation, as further supply shocks risk unanchoring
inflation expectations.
Nevertheless, one consequence of a world of more frequent supply shocks is that
actions are then taken to reduce vulnerabilities over the years ahead. For instance,
the 1970s oil shocks led to the widespread realisation that the US needed to

become less energy intensive and produce more of its own energy. As a result, the
Strategic Petroleum Reserve was set up, and the subsequent years saw the US
eventually become a net energy exporter. Similarly today, the closure of the Strait
of Hormuz has forced other countries to secure alternative supply routes and use
strategic reserves. So even before the longer-term geopolitical outcome is clear, we
have seen diversification away from one particular chokepoint. In a supply-driven

world, the incentives will shift towards making those supply chains more resilient,
and better able to withstand future shocks.

量卻逆勢年增約 15%至5,580萬支 ，市占率也從去
年同期的16.3%上升至20.1%，顯示iPhone 17減去
128GB機型提升平均售價 、下放部分旗艦機功能至
iPhone 17以帶動銷售量的策略持續奏效。

另一方面 ，小米、OPPO、vivo等中國品牌手機出
貨量在2Q26則年減26.3%、17.5%、19.4%，市佔
率全數下滑 ，顯示在記憶體價格持續攀升的情況下 ，
中國OEM品牌對中低階手機出貨採取較保守的策略 。
此 狀 況 也 對 高 通 手 機 營 收 造 成 影 響 ， 高通
FY26Q3(2Q26)手機營收年減 19.6%至50.9億美元 。
展望本季，蘋果預估營收年增率為 9%~11%，低於
市場預期的 12%，因外匯逆風及零組件供應限制加
劇對營收造成壓力；另外 ，本季毛利率預估下滑至
47%～48%，顯示記憶體成本壓力增加 。受記憶體
漲價影響，預估蘋果在9月發表iPhone 18時將上調
全系列新品售價100~300美元。由於新品售價大幅
上調，預估明年iPhone銷售數量將較今年下滑 ，但
ASP提升將抵銷部分不利因素；整體而言 ，預估
2027年iPhone營收年成長率將放緩至個位數百分比。
資料來源：高盛，2026/7/31，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/8/26

憶體(HBM)與伺服器記憶體 DDR5、NAND，以及

營收仍將成長 3倍以上 、不過季增已經縮小到 25-
35%。硬碟公司西方數據 、希捷科技同樣受益於漲
價效應 ，預估3Q26營收將續創新高 。工業用晶片
受到AI基礎建設對電源管理與感測元件的強勁需求 ，

開始復甦，預估2Q26有望年增10-20%。智慧手機
晶片處境最為艱辛 ，高通預估將年減10%。聯發科
則是有客製化 IC加持下抵銷手機晶片業績衰退 ，
3Q26營收展望持平。

5
《前沿模型資安事件反應網安實務升級的迫切性》
OpenAI、Anthropic、Meta 近期接連坦承旗下 AI
模型爆發資安事件。OpenAI 於 7/21 證實其模型涉
及Hugging Face平台遭入侵事件，並利用零日漏洞
取得存取權限。華盛頓郵報於 7/30 進一步披露該
公司測試用AI代理入侵他公司系統的完整時間軸，
顯示該代理行為具備相當程度的自主性與複雜度。

Anthropic 於 7/31 接露旗下 Claude 模型因測試環
境權限誤設定，意外取得網路存取權限並入侵三家
企業系統。Meta 則於 8/6 揭露，其 Muse Spark
1.1 模型於測試過程中同樣意外駭入另一家公司系
統。三起事件顯示當模型被賦予更廣泛的系統存取
與網路連線能力以進行更貼近真實場景的能力測試
時，「受控測試」與「未預期真實世界行動」之間
的界線正變得模糊，且此現象並非個別工程疏失，
而是隨模型能力躍升而必然浮現的結構性挑戰，可
說是資安領域的一個重要時點。這些事件讓企業得
以窺見網安威脅行為在短期內演變的路徑，有望驅
動企業以前所未有的速度從根本上提升網安實務，
持續支持本行看多網安軟體類股的中長期看法。然
而，投資人須注意的是，網安類股在過去數個月已
大幅地反映這個利多。本行仍認為目前股價已提前
反映基本面，建議靜待 8 月下旬網安類股的財報結
果再行動作。
小型平台 (AVPT, CKHP, OKTA, S, ZS)
領導平台 (CRWD, FTNT, NET, PANW)

重點摘要
零售客流量表
• 私募基金 Silver Lake 收購
Workday 傳言值得關注是否
為應用軟體建立估值底部。
• 應用軟體股估值與股價表現持

體。

6
《私募基金收購傳言值得關注是否為應用軟體建立
估值底部》
路透社於 8/13 獨家報導，私募基金 Silver Lake 正
與人力資源暨財務軟體業者 Workday (WDAY) 洽談
收購事宜。消息傳出後帶動 WDAY 等被「AI 吞噬
SaaS」敘事壓抑定價的應用軟體股價大漲。與此同
時，摩根大通近期報告指出應用軟體目前的EV/營

收乘數僅約當基礎設施軟體的三分之一，整體 SaaS
族群約 4x 的EV/營收乘數，已貼近 2008年金融海
嘯低點的 1.5x，遠低於 2020 年疫情高峰約 20x。
2026年迄今，基礎設施軟體與應用軟體股價的分化
持續擴大。然而，從各家主要應用軟體的財報表現
觀察，ServiceNow (NOW) 年增率趨穩，AI 年度合
約金額(ACV) 突破10億美元。Salesforce (CRM)、
Adobe (ADBE) 等亦積極將代理型 AI 功能嵌入核心
產品線，推動營收結構自席位制轉向用量制。儘管
目前仍難以斷言應用軟體是否將因生成式 AI 導致的
去中介化而面臨長期成長困境，但短期成長動能實
際上並未如敘事般崩塌。本行認為 Silver Lake 的收
購傳聞若屬實，將是以真金白銀對「應用軟體遭過
度錯殺」的判斷投下信任票，將成為檢視應用軟體
類股是否存在估值底部的重要指標，建議投資人可
持續追蹤相關動態與應用軟體財報結果以把握潛在
的反轉契機。
資料來源：摩根大通，2026/8/13，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/19

《谷歌、臉書短期催化劑有限 ，指數上行空間有限 》
通訊服務指數兩大權值股谷歌與臉書雖在 7 月底財
報時繳出強勁表現，但在 ROIC 持續回落時仍指出
2027 年資本支出仍將顯著成長仍為通訊指數帶來壓
力。而谷歌 8 月再次經歷高階人才出走、谷歌旗艦
模型與臉書 AI 投資變現遲未明朗更為指數上行增添
壓力。隨著資本支出仍將積極成長 ，市場在接下來
勢必以更嚴格角度檢視資本支出效率與變現進度 。
雖然本行認為谷歌 、臉書基本面短期仍具一定韌性 ，
臉書法律尾端風險大幅收斂 、谷歌輕量模型的變現

路徑持續拓寬 ，但觀察市場估值 ，顯示兩公司仍在
承壓。短期沒有新的催化劑下 ，指數上行動能仍較
有限。因此，本行對通訊服務指數的看法維持中立 。
• 繼 7 月流失 Gemini 大模型與
前沿技術的核心研發靈魂人物
後，谷歌高階技術人才出走潮
仍在繼續。
• 臉書 167 億美元和解兒少安全
與社群成癮訴訟，最大單一尾
部風險解除，然歐洲可能面臨
新的監管挑戰。
• 谷歌模型應用場景深化，但旗
艦模型進展仍持滯；臉書開源
與代理人雙軌並進，但變現時
程仍未明。
1
資產 3Q26 看法 投資展望
通訊服務 中立 通訊服務主成分谷歌與臉書短期承壓。谷歌流失 AI 人才，或已影響
到模型發布延後及研發進度放緩，臉書 AI 投資變現仍未明朗。在預
期 2027 資本支出仍將顯著成長的預期下，市場在接下來勢必以更嚴
格角度檢視資本支出效率與變現進度。短期催化劑不明限制兩權值股
的上行動能，也限制了指數的上行空間，維持評等為中立。
資料來源：Koyfin、Capital IQ，2026/08/31；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/31。

2
《繼流失 Gemini 核心人才，谷歌又見大將離職》
谷歌首席科學家 Jeff Dean 宣布離職當日谷歌股價
重挫近 5%，反映市場對其重要性的認知。Dean 在
谷歌任職 27 年，是公司搜尋引擎誕生初期即參與核
心架構設計的元老級人物：他主導共同設計了奠定
谷歌雲端與資料處理能力的分散式系統，其後領導
團隊開發 TensorFlow，並在近年出任公司首席科學
家，長期被視為谷歌工程文化的代表人。與 Dean

同步離職的另三人同樣具備不可替代性：資深研究
員 Sanjay Ghemawat 與 Dean 資歷相當，長年密
切合作，被《連線》雜誌譽為「網路時代最重要的
軟體工程師之一」；Oriol Vinyals 現任 Google
DeepMind 研究副總裁，是 Gemini 模型家族的技
術共同負責人；Quoc Le 為 Google Brain 創始成員
之一，2014 年的研究奠定了現今大型語言模型訓練
範式的重要源頭。四人合計代表谷歌過去二十餘年
在分散式運算、深度學習與生成式 AI 三個世代的技
術資產核心，此次集體出走並共同創辦專注科學與
工程領域 AI 應用的新創公司 Discovery Loop，雖

谷歌能否於前沿模型能力上維持對 OpenAI、
Anthropic 的競爭力，人才流失的訊號意義不容忽

2026 年 9 月9 日 全 3 頁

一時 152 円台に突入したドル円レート、日本
経済への影響と円高の背景 
10 円の円高ドル安でGDP は▲0.16％も多くの企業・家計にはプラス

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 9 月初めに 1 ドル＝160 円台だったドル円レートは一時152 円台まで円高ドル安が進ん
だ。10 円の円高ドル安は実質GDP を 0.16％程度押し下げるが、主因はインバウンド需
要の減少や負の資産効果で、 輸入物価の低下などを通じて多くの企業や家計にとって
はプラスとみられる。経済全体の観点から望ましいのは、円高そのものではなく、相
対的な物価や金利などのファンダメンタルズ（経済の基礎的条件）に沿った安定的な
為替相場だ。歴史的な円安の修正が緩やかに進むのであれば、企業収益への影響を抑
えつつ、物価上昇圧力を和らげ、内需の持続性を高めることが期待される。 
◼ ドル円レートの動きは日米の実質金利差で説明されることが多いが、2025 年秋以降は
両者の関係が薄れ、実質金利差が縮小傾向にある中で円安ドル高が進んだ。 だが、直
近では日銀の利上げ加速観測が強まり、日本の金利面からの円高圧力が高まったこと
で、ドル円レートと実質金利差との関係が再び強まった可能性がある。仮に2025 年夏
頃まで見られた両者の関係が回復すると、ドル円レートは1 ドル＝140 円台を割り込む
可能性もあるだろう。

10 円の円高ドル安は実質GDP を0.16％押し下げるも、多くの企業や家計にとってはプラス 
2026 年 9 月初めに 1 ドル＝160 円台だったドル円レートは、9 月 8 日に一時 152 円台をつけ
た。日本銀行（日銀）の利上げ加速観測や、日米当局が円安抑止に向けて追加的な対応を講じ
るとの見方などを背景に、円高ドル安が急速に進んだとみられる。 
当社の「第 230 回日本経済予測（改訂版）」（2026 年 9 月 8 日）の図表 2-9 左では、円安が平
時の日本経済に及ぼす影響についてマクロモデルを利用して試算したが、円高の場合はこの結
果をおおむね反転させたものとして整理できる。 
図表 1 は 10 円（変化率では 6％超）の円高ドル安による平時の日本経済への影響を試算した
結果で、実質 GDP を 0.16％程度押し下げる。近年は円安局面でも輸出数量が増えにくく、生産
や設備投資への波及効果は限定的であるため、円高が進んだ場合も 財輸出への影響は小さいと

### Page 2

1

2 / 3

みられる（寄与度は▲0.01％pt 程度）。影響が大きいのは、サービス輸出の減少（インバウン
ド需要の減少、同▲0.11％pt 程度）と、負の資産効果（株安を通じた個人消費の抑制 、同▲
0.09％pt 程度）だ。一方、輸入物価の低下は実質GDP を押し上げる（同＋0.05％pt 程度）。 
インバウンド需要減の影響は主として観光関連産業に集中する。家計についても、金融資産
の多くを高齢世帯や高所得世帯が保有しているため、中低所得の勤労世帯などでの 負の資産効
果は小さい。そのため、多くの企業や家計にとっては輸入物価の 低下を通じた購買力の改善に
よるプラスの効果の方が大きいだろう。実質雇用者報酬への影響は＋0.1％程度で、円高はマ
クロで見た雇用者の所得環境を改善させるとみられる。 
図表 1：10 円の円高ドル安による日本経済への影響

（注）大和総研の短期マクロモデルによる試算結果。ドル円レートが 160 円/ドル（2026 年 9 月初めの水準に相
当）から 10 円（6.3％）の円高ドル安を想定。 
（出所）各種統計より大和総研作成

足元で円高が急速に進んだとはいえ、水準で見ればなお円安だ。円安基調が急速に強まった
のは 2022 年春で、同年 3 月初めのドル円レートは1 ドル＝115 円程度だった。また、通貨の総
合的な価値を表す実質実効為替レートで見ると、直近の2026 年 7 月には遡及可能な 1970 年 1
月以降の最低水準を更新した。8 月以降は円高方向にシフトしたとみられるものの、現在も歴
史的な円安局面にあることに変わりはない。 
経済全体の観点から望ましいのは、円高そのものではなく、相対的な物価や金利などのファ
ンダメンタルズ（経済の基礎的条件）に沿った安定的な為替相場である。急激な円高は輸出企
業や観光関連産業への調整圧力を強める一方、急激な円安は輸入インフレを通じて家計の購買
力を低下させる。歴史的な円安の修正が緩やかに進むのであれば、企業収益への影響を抑えつ
つ、物価上昇圧力を和らげ、内需の持続性を高めることが期待される。

2025 年夏頃まで見られたドル円レートと金利差の関係が回復すれば1 ドル＝140 円台割れも

3 / 3

関係が薄れ、実質金利差が縮小傾向にある中で円安ドル高が進んだ。 
背景には、日本の金利上昇によるドル円レートの円高圧力が限定的だったことが影響した可
能性がある。図表 2 には日米金利差の米国要因と日本要因（逆目盛）をそれぞれ折れ線グラフ
で示したが、日本の金利変動がドル円レートに及ぼす影響は米国よりも小さく、日本の金利上
昇局面でも円安ドル高が進んだ。金利水準が物価上昇率などに比して低く（たとえば、短期か
ら中期の実質金利はマイナス圏） 、経済実態を踏まえた金利水準（中立金利）を下回っている
ことなどが影響したとみられる。 
だが、直近では日銀の利上げ加速観測が強まり、日本の金利面からの円高圧力が 高まったこ
とで、ドル円レートと実質金利差との関係が再び強まった可能性がある。また、日米当局は 7
月31 日に協調為替介入を実施したが、今後も円安抑止へ対策を打つ可能性が燻っていることも
円高を後押ししているとみられる。 
当社では、米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）は 2027 年にかけて利下げを断続的に実施すること
を見込む一方1、日銀は 2026 年 9 月の金融政策決定会合で短期金利1.25％への引き上げを決め、
その後も四半期の一度程度のペースで追加利上げを続けるとみている（ 2027 年 1-3 月期に
1.75％に達した後は当面据え置き） 。中東情勢の影響で原油価格は高水準にあり、世界的なイ
ンフレ圧力は依然として強いことから特に米国の物価・金利見通しの不透明感は強いものの、
仮に2025 年夏頃まで見られたドル円レートと実質金利差の関係が回復すれば、ドル円レートは
1 ドル＝140 円台を割り込む可能性もあるだろう。

伊朗⾰命卫队称，⼀艘油轮试图通过霍尔⽊兹海峡南部 “ 禁航区域 ” ，触碰⽔雷后爆炸起⽕。
摘要：
据伊朗法尔斯通讯社 14 ⽇报道，伊朗伊斯兰⾰命卫队⽅⾯表⽰，⼀艘名为 “ 阿尔加亚 ” 的油轮试图
通过霍尔⽊兹海峡南部 “ 禁航区域 ” 时，触碰⽔雷后爆炸起⽕。
⾰命卫队称，事发后，相关⽅⾯尝试控制⽕势，但未能成功，⽬前整艘油轮已被⼤⽕吞没。
伊朗⽅⾯表⽰此前已就 “ ⾮法航道 ” 存在的危险发出警告，并强调霍尔⽊兹海峡仍在伊⽅管控下，
处于 “ 封锁状态 ” 。
⽬前暂⽆涉事油轮⼈员伤亡情况、船旗国以及载油量等更多信息。
本⽂来源：新华社，原⽂标题：《伊朗⾰命卫队：⼀艘油轮试图穿越海峡时触雷爆炸》

Document 524: 内外経済ウォッチ『日本～こじれる消費税減税の裏側にあるもの～』(2026年9月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『日本～こじれる消費税減税の裏側にあるもの～』(2026年9月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

日本
～こじれる消費税減税の裏側にあるもの～
食料品の消費税率を引き下げる政府方針が閣議決定
された。 2027年4月から税率を8％から1％へと引き下
げ、 中低所得者にはさらに1％分を給付することで実質ゼ
ロを実現すると している。 財源やインフレへの影響を懸念
する声も強く、 議論は百家争鳴の様相を呈している。
批判的な立場からは、 消費税減税の効果は小さいとの
指摘もある。 物価上昇下では減税分の一部が値上げに吸
収され、 家計の実感は乏しいのではないか、 との見方だ。
しかし、 物価上昇下であっても減税する場合と しない場合
の差は生じるはずで、 家計の実質所得を押し上げるだろ
う。 また、 給付や所得税減税と異なり、 消費税減税は 「消費
すること」 に対する減税である。 幅広く家計の負担を軽減
すると同時に、 支出を促し、 需要を喚起する効果も相対的
に大きい。 負担軽減策であると同時に、 景気刺激策と して
の性格も強いのである。
効果がないことが問題なのではない。 効果があるから

こそ課題が多いのである。 効果があるからこそ、 税率を
戻す際の家計への負担も大きくなる。 消費へのインセン
ティブを高める性質を踏まえても、 景気悪化時の需要喚
起策と して行うならば理解できる。 しかし、 足元ではイン
フレが定着し、 長期金利が上昇する中、 実質賃金もプラ
スに転じつつある。 そう した局面で需要刺激策によって
景気のアクセルを踏むことには、 やはり経済政策と して
のちぐはぐさが残る。
また、 今回の消費税減税は、 2年間の措置後に新設す
る給付付き税額控除へ接続するものと整理されている。
しかし、 所得に応じて支援する制度と、 消費額に応じて恩
恵が及ぶ制度では、 対象も政策効果も異なる。 前者は低
所得層への重点支援に向き、 後者は幅広い家計の負担
軽減と需要喚起に向く。 両者を代替関係と して扱うこと
には難しさがある。
百家争鳴の消費税減税
効果があるからこそ課題が多い
一方で、 足元で家計が 「割を食っている」 側面も否定は
できない。 暫定値ではあるが、 内閣府の最新財政試算で

は2025年度の政府のプライマリーバランスがおおむね
均衡する。 税収が急ピッチで増加していることなどが背
景にあるが、 それは物価上昇によって税収が増える一
方、 政府部門からの還元が十分に進んでいないことの裏
返しでもある。
筆者は、 議論がこ じれる根底には、 家計を支える ２ つの
仕組みの不在があると考えている。
１ つ目は、 インフレによって家計に生じる負担増を自動
的に調整する仕組みだ。 物価が上昇すると、 名目所得や消
費額の増加を通じて税収は増えやすい。 一方、 税制上の基
準や公的給付が物価に応じて改定されなければ、 家計の
実質的な負担は重くなる。
諸外国と同様に公的価格や給付水準を物価に応じて改
定するとともに、 所得税の課税最低限や税率区分を調整
するブラケッ ト ・ クリープ対策などを講じ、 インフレによる
「隠れ増税」 を一定程度相殺する必要がある。 筆者の試算
では、 2019→25年の物価上昇に控除等を調整しなかっ
たことによる隠れ増税額は年1.9兆円に上る。

２ つ目は、 所得や生活状況に応じて、 必要な家計に迅速
かつ柔軟に支援を届ける仕組みだ。 給付付き税額控除は、
その有力な選択肢である。 対象を絞るための所得情報
や、 迅速に給付するための行政基盤も含め、 平時から仕組
みを用意しておく必要がある。
この ２ つの仕組みが欠けているため、 家計の負担が高
まるたびに、 給付や減税が形を変えて議論される。 コロナ
禍の一律給付、 所得税の定額減税、 そして今回の消費税
減税と、 家計向け支援はその都度政治判断に委ねられ、 政
争の種となってきた。 ２ つの仕組みを整え、 その場その場
の給付 ・ 減税から脱却し、 政策の安定性を高めることが求
められている。
欠けている ２ つの仕組み
経済調査部　主席エコノミス ト　星野 卓也（ほしの たくや）

Document 621: 库克承认了！苹果是看华为等出折叠机才做的

Source PDF: all report pdf/库克承认了！苹果是看华为等出折叠机才做的.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

据外媒苹果⾸席记者古尔曼透露，苹果公司决定认真开发折叠屏的节点⼤约在 2020 年。当
时库克刚结束亚洲⾏，看到消费者开始使⽤华为、三星等竞品推出的折叠屏⼿机，因此确
信苹果需要推出⾃⼰的折叠屏⼿机。然⽽⽬前华为、三星等折叠机都已经过多次迭代，苹
果现在⼊场，很难说会抢⾛多少份额。
摘要：
今⽇，话题词 “ 库克承认是看华为等⼚家出折叠机才出的 ” 登上微博热搜。
据外媒苹果⾸席记者古尔曼透露，苹果公司决定认真开发折叠屏⼿机的节点⼤约在 2020 年前
后。当时，苹果前 CEO 库克刚结束亚洲⾏，回来后对折叠屏⼿机感到 “ 异常兴奋 ”—— 他在中国等
亚洲国家看到越来越多的消费者开始使⽤华为、三星等竞品推出的折叠屏⼿机，因此确信苹果
需要推出⾃⼰的折叠屏⼿机。
今年，距离三星发布初代折叠机已经七年。在过去七年时间⾥，三星折叠机都迭代到了第⼋
代，华为更是把折叠形态推进到了三折，⼩⽶、荣耀、 OPPO 也都各⾃推出了多代产品。苹果现
在⼊场，很难说会抢⾛多少份额。
违背祖训的⼿写笔
iPhone Duo 展开后 7.6 英⼨内屏，是 iPhone 史上最⼤的屏幕；折叠后 5.4 英⼨外屏，可单⼿操

作。两块屏幕均为 Super Retina XDR ，苹果称⼆者采⽤相同的显⽰⽐例，⽀持 ProMotion 、常亮
显⽰，峰值亮度达 3000 尼特。值得⼀提的是，内屏的前置摄像头隐藏在屏下，需要时才出现。
芯⽚是苹果最新的 A20 Pro 。存储从 256GB 起，另有 512GB 、 1TB 和 2TB 四档，售价分为 1999 ，
2199 ， 2599 和 3199 美元。最值得注意的⼀个差异化功能是，两块屏幕均⽀持 Apple Pencil （不
是 Pro 版，且要随后实现）。这是 iPhone ⾸次⽀持⼿写笔，也是当下少数提供触控笔⽀持的折叠
屏。
遥想当年 iPhone 发布会的时候，乔布斯那句著名的嘲讽 “ 既然有了⼿指，谁还需要⼿写笔 ” ，当然
时代变了，产品形态和使⽤场景也变了，库克都出了⼤屏⼿机，特纳斯也没必要坚守乔布斯的
祖训对吧？
价格是故意定在 1999 美元的。据最了解苹果的媒体⼈⻢克 · 古尔曼（ Mark Gurman ）报道，苹果
早就把⽬标定在 1,999 美元，希望守住 “2,000 美元这道可能引发争议的⼼理⻔槛 ” ，以复刻 2017

年 iPhone X 以 999 美元登场的策略。虽然在全球内存短缺的压⼒下，苹果内部⼀度讨论过将定价
上调到 2199 美元的⽅案。但最终苹果还是坚持 1999 美元的既定⽅案，决定⾃⼰吞下部分成本，
也押注更多⽤⼾会选择⾼利润的⼤容量版本。
哑光涂层遮掩折痕
虽然苹果折叠机是最后上市的，但苹果并不需要明显优于竞争对⼿，更不需要具有价格优势，
他们只需要满⾜⾃⼰⽤⼾对折叠机的渴望。来看看主要竞争对⼿的折叠机，就会清楚地显⽰这
⼀点：
三星 Galaxy Z Fold8 展开厚度 4.5 毫⽶，重 201 克，官⽅称为 “ 全球最轻折叠屏 ” ，售价 1899 美元
起， Z Fold8 Ultra 为 2,099 美元。

### Page 2

刚刚发布的⼩⽶ 18 Fold 展开厚度 5.02 毫⽶，重 219 克，雷军在发布会上直接称其⽐ iPhone Pro
Max 更轻，售价⼈⺠币 10999 元⾄ 14999 元。
华为 Mate XT2 三折设计，展开接近平板尺⼨，重新设计的铰链、更强防⽔和防窥屏，整体性能
较上代提升 42% ，定价更为⾼端，售价 19999 元起，顶配 24999 元。
⽽据《连线》杂志报道， iPhone Duo 重量约 235 克，是这四家之中最重的⼀个。苹果更选择了不
同于竞争对⼿的折叠形态，做的是最基础的书本式对折。⽽且，苹果还在发布时嘲讽了直板折
叠形态并不实⽤，似乎并不打算给⾃⼰未来推出类似产品留下余地。
不过，苹果处理折痕的⼿段引发了热议。折痕是折叠屏七年来最顽固的问题，苹果的解法出⼈
意料地朴素，在主屏幕上覆盖⼀层特殊的纳⽶纹理涂层，通过漫射光线，让折痕在视觉上消
失。美国媒体评价， “ 你可以摸到折痕，却看不到它 ” 。
三星继续嘲讽苹果
和往常⼀样，苹果即便姗姗来迟，也做不到遥遥领先，但他们的⼊场总能改变了整个品类的市

场结构。 The Verge 直接表⽰， “ 苹果依然说了算 ” 。这个论断的依据不是技术，⽽是市场格局，
尤其是在严重缺乏竞争的美国市场，这⾥实际上只有苹果三星⾕歌摩托等四个⼿机品牌竞争。
苹果占据美国智能⼿机市场约 57% 的份额，三星只有 25% 。 IDC 此前预测，苹果折叠机⾸年出货
份额约 22% ，但预计均价将⾼达 2400 美元，将拿下 34% 的销售额。 Counterpoint 的预测更激

### Page 3

进，认为苹果将在北美市场直接拿⾛ 46% 的销售额。
三星显然对这个叙事很不服⽓。在苹果发布会前数⼩时，三星发布了⼀⽀名为《欢迎来到折叠
屏》的⼴告，强调 Z Fold8 是全球最轻的折叠屏。
三星研发主管⽂熙哲在接受《连线》采访时说得客⽓但意思明确： ” 看到这些品牌进⼊市场，我们
为⾃⼰感到骄傲。我们开创的这个新品类的价值，正在被承认和认可。 ” 他同时强调，重量是折叠
屏最关键的指标。
三星美国的社交账号则毫不客⽓，在发布会全程连发数条嘲讽苹果：把 iPhone 的设计⽐作《⼟
拨⿏之⽇》，讽刺其 ” 反复加热三星的剩饭 ” ，还调侃说 “ 我们向多邻国道歉 —— 苹果把你们的名字
也抄了 ” 。
中国市场竞争太激烈
这场发布会留下了两个问题，可能会影响到苹果在中国的市场前景。
第⼀， Siri AI 没有中国。
AI 重⽣版 Siri 由⾕歌 Gemini 模型驱动，将随 9 ⽉ 14 ⽇的 iOS 27 推出。但⾸发范围明确排除了两个
市场：欧盟 27 国和中国。同时新 Siri 仅⽀持英语，其他语⾔（法语、⽇语、韩语、葡萄⽛语、西

班⽛语）要到 10 ⽉才陆续开放。
这个 AI 功能缺席的影响可能被低估了。苹果在中国市场的份额实际上已被华为反超，第⼆季度的
份额分别是 18.1% 和 22.6% ，⽽华为有鸿蒙和⾃研麒麟芯⽚，⼩⽶有澎湃 OS 和端侧模型。中国
消费者对⼿机 AI 能⼒的期待值，恰恰是全球最⾼的市场之⼀。⼀部售价近 1.5 万元⼈⺠币的折叠
旗舰，如果最核⼼的 AI 功能在中国不可⽤，可能会让⼀些⾼端⽤⼾决定持币待购。
第⼆，从发布到发货隔了⼀个半⽉。
苹果 9 ⽉ 9 ⽇发布折叠机，但直到 10 ⽉ 23 ⽇才上市。这是苹果⾃ 2017 年 iPhone X 以来最⻓的⼀次
发布到发货间隔：当年的 iPhone X 在 9 ⽉ 12 ⽇发布，直到 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇才发货。⽽ iPhone 18 Pro 系列
的节奏⼀如往常，并没有受到影响。
苹果没有解释原因。古尔曼此前的报道提到，这款设备的⼯程复杂度可能限制初期供应。折叠
屏的良率问题是⾏业公开的难题：铰链、超薄玻璃、屏下摄像头，每⼀项都会压低良率，苹果

还专⻔开发了新的纳⽶涂层⼯艺，以解决对⽆折痕的执着。

### Page 4

低端机型集体缺席
这场发布会最反常的地⽅，是苹果没发布的东西。
往年秋季，苹果会同时推出基础版和 Pro 版。今年秋天只有 iPhone 18 Pro 、 Pro Max 和 Duo 三
款，全部是⾼端机型。基础版 iPhone 18 、⼊⻔级 iPhone 18e 和第⼆代 iPhone Air ，全部推迟到
2027 年春季。这是苹果历史上第⼀次推迟基础款 iPhone 的发布。
据《⽇经亚洲》援引多位消息⼈⼠的说法，这⼀调整 “ 意在优化资源配置，在存储芯⽚价格飙升
的背景下，最⼤化⾼端机型的营收与利润 ” 。同⼀批消息⼈⼠还提到另⼀个原因：这对苹果 “ 在量
产⾸款折叠 iPhone 时尽量减少潜在的⽣产波动⾄关重要 ” 。
两个原因指向同⼀件事：内存不够⽤了，所以要把有限的产能优先分配给⽑利最⾼的产品。
这个判断在另⼀个细节上得到印证。分析师郭明錤此前指出， iPhone 18 的内存可能只升级到
9GB ⽽⾮ 12GB ，但是 iOS 27 中 Siri 的语⾳定制和听写功能，恰恰需要 12GB 内存才能运⾏。换句

话说，内存危机正在直接决定哪些机型能⽤上 AI 功能。
⻛险是显⽽易⻅的。基础款 iPhone ⼀直是全球销量最⼤的机型， iPhone 17 ⾄今仍是全球最畅销
的智能⼿机。放弃⼀整个秋季销售旺季的⼊⻔市场，等于把这部分需求让给了安卓阵营，或者
让给了⾃⼰的旧机型。
除了 iPhone 18 Pro 和 Pro Max 较上⼀代上调 100 美元。真正罕⻅的是，已经在售的旧机型也被提
价。 iPhone 17 从 799 美元涨⾄ 899 美元， iPhone Air 涨⾄ 1099 美元⾸次进⼊四位数区间， iPhone
17e 涨⾄ 699 美元，连更早的 iPhone 16 也涨到了 799 美元。 iPhone 17 Pro 则直接停产。
苹果给出的理由是内存和存储成本上涨。库克此前曾把这轮半导体短缺形容为 “ 百年⼀遇的洪
⽔ ” 。把这两件事放在⼀起看，苹果的策略其实相当清晰：⽤更少的机型、更⾼的单价，在产能
受限的情况下守住利润。它宁愿牺牲销量，也要保住那道 50% 的⽑利率。

这是⼀个有代价的选择。在中国市场，苹果的份额已经被华为反超（第⼆季度 18.1% 对
22.6% ）；⽽涨价发⽣在⼀个消费趋于谨慎、本⼟品牌产品⼒⼤幅追平的环境⾥。⽽苹果显然选
择了冒险，把筹码押注在了⾼端市场。
AI 依然是个未知数
把这三条线索放在⼀起，苹果这次⼊场的图景其实相当清晰。
在美国， “ 最后⼊场却收割 ” 的剧本⼤概率会重演。 57% 的存量⽤⼾、成熟的换机通道，以及⼀个
没有华为⼩⽶的市场，这三个条件⾜以⽀撑那个 40% 的份额预测。三星做了七年，最后也只能
看着苹果卖得盆满钵满。
但在中国，这套剧本的前提并不成⽴。 2014 年苹果做⼤屏时，中国没有⼀个份额超过它的本⼟
对⼿；今天华为在中国折叠屏市场占据约 68% 的出货量，且整体份额已经反超苹果。加上新版
Siri 的缺席， Duo 在中国⾯对的是⼀场完全不同的竞争场景。
更根本的问题在于，折叠屏本⾝是⼀个出货很少的超⾼端市场。 Counterpoint 的数据显⽰，全球
折叠屏出货量⽬前仅占智能⼿机总量的约 2% ，⽽ 2026 年上半年出货量甚⾄同⽐下滑了 21.6% ，

仅为 510 万部。就算苹果可以带动市场整体规模增⻓超过三成，依然是智能⼿机中的极⼩众市
场。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

管环境，⽬前⽆法预知真正可⽤时间表。折叠屏可能是苹果最后⼀个能靠⼯业设计和渠道优势

Source PDF: all report pdf/強含みする豪ドル､追加利上げ観測が引き続き堅調さを促すか ～RBAの｢タカ派｣姿勢の後押し材料は多く､ワーホリ規制の動きが影響する可能性も～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-08-26
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 8 月 26 日

1/3 
強含みする豪ドル､追加利上げ観測が引き続き堅調さを促すか 
～RBA の｢タカ派｣姿勢の後押し材料は多く､ワーホリ規制の動きが影響する可能性も～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 豪ドル相場は、日本と米国による協調介入や米国のバイバック倍増表明を受けた米ドル安に
加え、ＲＢＡの｢タカ派｣姿勢を背景に強含んでいる。ＲＢＡは８月会合で政策金利を２会合
連続で据え置いたものの、ブロック総裁はインフレの上振れリスクを警戒し、必要に応じて
追加利上げに動く可能性を示した。８月会合の議事要旨でも利上げの是非が議論され、一部
委員が追加利上げの必要性に言及するなど、先行きの金融引き締め観測がくすぶる。 
○ ７月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.5％と鈍化したものの、13 ヵ月連続でＲＢＡの目標レンジ （２
～３％） を上回った。 コアインフレ率も同＋3.6％と高止まりしており、 前月比では上昇ペー
スが加速するなど、基調的なインフレ圧力の根強さが確認された。原油高によるエネルギー
価格や食料品価格の上昇も重なり、生活必需品を中心に物価上昇圧力が続いている。 
○ 先行きも、イラン情勢の長期化による原油高に加え、エルニーニョ現象による異常気象が農
作物生産を下押しする可能性がある。さらに、ワーキングホリデービザを巡る制約強化によ
る農業部門の労働需給ひっ迫も、物価上昇圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。このため、ＲＢＡ
による追加利上げが意識されやすく、豪ドル相場は当面、堅調に推移する可能性が高い。

このところの金融市場では、 豪ドルが強
含みする展開が続いて いる。日本と米国
による協調介入 に加え、米国によるバイ
バック （長期国債の買い入れ消却） の倍増
表明を受け、その後の米ドル指数が 軟調
な動きを強めたことも追い風に、足元の
豪ドル相場は２ヵ月半ぶりの高値圏で推
移している。 日本円に対しても、 協調介入
を受けて大幅に調整する動きがみられた
ものの、足元では協調介入前の水準をう
かがうまでに戻すなど、急速に持ち直 す
図 1 豪ドル相場(対米ドル､日本円)の推移

（出所）LSEG より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 2

2/3 
動きがみられる （図１） 。背景には、 ＲＢＡ （オーストラリア準備銀行） が依然として 「タカ派」 姿
勢を維持していることがある。 ＲＢＡは８月会合で政策金利を２会合連続で据え置いたものの、 会
合後の記者会見において、 ブロック総裁はインフレの上振れリスクを警戒し、 必要に応じて追加利
上げに動く可能性に言及した （注１）。さらに、 ＲＢＡによる断続的な利上げ実施に加え、 不動産価
格抑制を目的とする政府の税制改革の影響も重なり、 足元の不動産価格は下落基調に転じるなど変
化の兆しがみられる。こうした状況について、ブロック氏は政策運営の主眼ではないうえ、金融安
定に対するリスクとはならないと述べた。 
その後の金融市場では、 ＲＢＡによる追加利上げが意識されるとともに、 豪ドル相場が強含みす
る動きをみせている。８月 25 日に公表された８月会合の議事要旨によれば、会合では利上げの是
非が議論され、 数人の委員がインフレの上振れリスクを勘案して追加利上げが必要になる可能性に
言及した。一方で、他の委員は、過去の利上げが効果をあげており、経済動向を見極めるべく「あ
る程度の時間」を割く余裕があると判断した。そして、利上げの是非を協議した結果、全会一致で
金利据え置きを決定する一方、インフレ率を妥当な期間のうちに目標レンジ（２～３％）に収束さ
せることへの確信を強める追加情報が今後の判断に有用との認識で一致した。 なお、2024 年の中銀
法改正により、ＲＢＡの金融政策は、物価安定と完全雇用の維持という「デュアルマンデート」に
基づいて運営されている。このため、市場では物価統計と雇用統計の動きが注目される。７月の雇
用統計は想定外に弱含む動きが確認されたものの （注２）、月次の雇用統計は変動が大きいうえ、 今
回も過去の数値が遡及改定されており、単月の結果には幅を持ってみる必要がある。 
こうしたなか、 ７月のインフレ率は前年同月比＋3.5％と前月 （同＋3.8％） から鈍化したものの、
13 ヵ月連続で目標レンジを上回る伸びで推移していることが確認された（図２）。 前月比は＋1.02％
と前月（同▲0.06％）から３ヵ月ぶりの
上昇に転じ、 原油高の再燃によるエネル
ギー価格の上昇のほか、 生鮮品をはじめ
とする食料品価格も上昇しており、生活
必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まっ
ている。コアインフレ率（トリム平均ベ
ース） は前年同月比＋3.6％と前月 （同＋
3.6％） から２ヵ月連続の横ばいで、10 ヵ
月連続で目標レンジを上回る伸びで推
移している。前月比も＋ 0.49％と前月
（同＋0.28％）から上昇ペースが加速し

注１ ８月12 日付レポート「RBA は想定以上に｢タカ派｣､豪ドル相場は強含みする可能性も」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/645176.html） 
注２ ８月20 日付レポート「雇用悪化でRBA の早期利上げ観測後退も､豪ドル相場はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654381.html） 
図 2 インフレ率の推移

（出所）CEIC より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
ており、財・サービスとも、さらに財価格も貿易財・非貿易財ともに上昇圧力がかかっている。な
お、 月次の物価統計のうちＲＢＡが比較的注視してきた物価変動の大きい財と観光を除いたベース
は前年同月比＋3.8％と、前月 （同＋4.2％） から鈍化して７ヵ月ぶりの低い伸びとなった。 ただし、
前月比は＋0.79％と前月（同＋0.02％）から上昇ペースが加速しており、インフレ圧力の根強さが
うかがえる。
コアインフレの高止まりが確認されたことを受けて、 ＲＢＡが懸念するインフレリスクの高さが
警戒される。さらに、足元ではイラン情勢の長期化による原油高が長引くなど、エネルギーインフ
レが続くことが懸念される。加えて、エルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、東部・北部では高温・乾

燥による少雨、南部では高温となる可能性が高く、主力作物である小麦・大麦・菜種などの生産に
悪影響が出ることも考えられる。 折しも、 ワーキングホリデービザの発給を巡る制約が強まるなか、
こうした労働力への依存度が高い農業部門では、労働需給のひっ迫が生産の制約要因となるほか、
物価上昇圧力を増幅させる可能性もある。 このため、 先行きもＲＢＡによる追加利上げが意識され
やすく、豪ドル相場は堅調な動きをみせることが考えられる。

月全國CPI偏上行將強化日銀升息動機 ，9/11利率期貨
市場預期9月升息1碼機率97%，12月再升1碼機率67%。
➢ 美國近期就業強勁且7月受亞馬遜Prime Day高基期而放
緩影響不再 ，8月零售銷售動能反彈 。中國政治局指示
加快財政支出，提供消費補貼或將挹注後續零售動能。
➢ 美國油氣鑽探活動活躍且延續高產能利用率 ，帶動8月
工業生產動能 。AI相關技術生產將仍挹注中國工業生產
動能，然延續性待政府政策推動 。衡量日本民間設備投
資領先指標機器訂單受惠製造業需求而持續增長。

➢ 8月底日本財務省外匯存底 1.208兆美元 ，月減6.18%，
減幅創歷來新高 ，因7/30~8/26日本財務省動用 15.4
兆日圓干預措施以及殖利率上升導致政府公債價值跌
落。近期日圓反彈 ，市場推測可能因匯率檢查 ，被市
場視為準備干預匯市的前奏。
➢ 日銀官員近日釋出鷹派訊號，Q2GDP較初估值1.1%上
修1.4%、日本7月名目現金薪資年增 4.7%，創1997年
以來最大升幅，進一步強化市場對9月例會升息預期。
高市經濟顧問會田卓司預測日銀後續將加快升息速度 ，
被解讀為鴿派立場高市政府凝聚以升息抑制貶值的共
識，9/11利率期貨市場預期 12月有67%機率再升 1碼。
➢ 此外，日本政府年金投資基金 GPIF可能增加國內資產
配置比重 ，小摩估若股債均拉高至配置上限 31%則共
有33.8兆日圓潛在買盤投入市場 。日圓干預風險猶存 、
升息預期增溫 ，短線日圓獲支撐 ，後續留意日銀利率

決策會議動向 ，預期本週日圓在 152-156間震盪走升 。

其中通訊 、金融公司 、保險、科技、公用事業和能源產
業殖利率高於平均，建議逢利率回彈時分批佈局。

等債殖利率超過 7%，而在通訊 、運輸和科技產業殖利率
在8%以上，相對其他產業債殖利率水準較高。

科技投等債看增，高殖利率具吸引力
10
目前投等債信用利差仍相對窄
圖表解讀
➢ 儘管美國公債殖利率攀升 ，然企業寧可在目前偏高的
利率環境 ，趁著信用利差仍窄時發債 ，而不願在未來
債券供給將持續增加的預期下 ，承擔借貸成本更貴或
更難預測的風險 ，促使更多公司提前進行融資計畫 。
根據彭博調查，交易商預估9月美國投資級公司債發行
量將創同期新高達到 2,150至2,500億美元 ，而高盛則
預期2026年投等債發債量達2.3兆美元。
➢ AI基建題材下推動科技相關 (科技+通訊業)投等債發債
量逐年提升，今年前8月科技投等債超過去年全年發債
量。目前美國投資級公司債平均殖利率突破 5.5%，科
技、通訊投等債高於平均水準 ，建議擇優挑選資本支
出高但市場高度認可獲利能力的科技巨頭債券 ，以鎖
定高票息與穩健現金流 ，而若想均衡配置資產降低資
產波動度，亦可佈局於iShares投等債ETF(LQD)。

韓國廠在中系為主的電池產業殺出重圍
13
圖表解讀
➢ 韓國研究機構 SNE Research數據顯示 2026年1至7
月全球動力電池裝車總量年增 20.4%至725.2GWh，
其中韓系電池領頭羊 LG能源排名第三 ，韓國三大廠
(LG能源、SK On、三星SDI)全球市佔率13~14%。
➢ 隨韓廠事業結構多元化 ，拓展至儲能系統 、資料中
心用不斷電系統與電池備援單元等非電動車領域策
略，韓國電池三雄近期業績反彈。像三星SDI目標次
世代技術全固態電池於 2027年下半年量產 ，而擁有
硫化物基固體電解質專利與技術的美國新創廠 Solid
Power技轉SK On，並輸出材料與三星SDI合作生產。
➢ 中國電池廠擴張受美國通膨削減法案IRA與歐盟碳排
規範防堵 ，相比韓國電池廠與歐美車廠間長期合作
以及完善綠色管理體系 (與回收商建立循環供應鏈 )
而享歐美市場准入優勢 ，LG能源符合美國通膨削減
法取得先進製造生產稅額抵免等 。韓國三大電池廠
和相關企業可關注iShares MSCI南韓ETF(EWY)。

AI派對尚未結束，距泡沫破滅還早

圖表解讀
➢ 科技巨頭普遍持續加碼投資以鞏固 AI算力優勢 ，市

➢ Raymond James提出「25法則」，根據美國250年
歷史經驗 ，新技術出現 (如鐵路 、網路熱潮 )時企業
大量發債或印股票籌錢 ，當單一產業吸收相當於經
濟產出量能約 25%投向變革性技術支出 ，使資金高
度集中於單一產業 ，導致其他實體經濟因借不到錢 ，
使泡沫承受不住而崩塌 ，然在破滅後仍為經濟和社
會帶來實體和數位基礎建設。
➢ 近兩年美國投入 AI支出約2兆美元(包含2024年至今
買AI 晶片 5,000 億美元 、 晶片運作電力基礎設施
3,500億美元、資料中心建築費2,000億美元)，推估
以 目 前 擴 張 速 度 約 到2030 年 代 初 期 才 會 觸 頂，
Raymond James認為現在距離泡沫破裂還早得很。

小麥供應趨緊，全球糧食危機恐迫在眉梢
17資料來源：USAD, 2026
股
票
市
場
地緣政治衝突、極端氣候壓抑小麥產銷
全球小麥市場規模持續攀升
圖表解讀
➢ 全球逾35%人口核心主食是小麥 。全球小麥出口龍頭
俄羅斯(佔全球貿易量 1/5)和黑海糧倉烏克蘭近期互襲
出口設施與商業船舶 ，地緣政治惡化致物流嚴重受阻 。
俄羅斯出口商將海運糧食改經鐵路轉往波羅的海港口
(如拉脫維亞 、愛沙尼亞)，然因年處理上限僅約 700萬
噸，無法填補黑海每年4,600萬噸的巨大缺口。
➢ 傳統小麥出口主力歐盟(尤其法國、德國)今年夏季受歷
史性高溫衝擊產量，聖嬰現象導致南半球(澳洲、巴西)
小麥產量大減 。機構預測全球今年秋冬逾 90%機率迎
來極強聖嬰現象 ，引發主食價格上漲 ，消費者轉向其
他地區種植食品 ，推高未經歷極端氣候地區產品價格 。
➢ 大宗商品諮詢機構 Expana展望2026/27年全球主要小
麥出口國的庫存消費比預估將降至 16.4%是歷史數據
中最低水平之一 。當小麥等農產品因糧食緊缺而價格

飆漲，將推升肥料 、農機到跨國糧商等供應鏈利潤 。
相關標的關注Invesco DB農業ETF(DBA)。

### Page 19

8月機會市場亮點
19
機
會
市
場
資產別 市場 亮點
股票
市場
美國 AI題材帶動長期產業需求與企業資本支出支撐大盤。今年以來標普趨勢走高，顯示市場面對消息面
震盪時具抗壓與修復能力。即便美國期中選舉帶來短期不確定性，但不確定性消彌後市場或迎反彈。
中國 中國推動科技自立自強，數據中心建設與新能源需求帶動原材料類股表現，硬體科技與AI供應鏈受
關注，成分散美股擁擠交易的替代選擇。相較歐美股市高估值，中國估值相對偏低具投資吸引力。
新亞 全球AI基礎建設帶動台、韓等半導體與供應鏈規格持續升級，帶動經濟成長預期優於成熟市場。
科技 科技巨頭持續加碼資本支出，對於AI伺服器、先進晶片需求具韌性，相關供應鏈訂單保持高檔。
Apple新品發表會題材，如新一代iPhone等重點產品可望帶動科技硬體與相關概念股買氣。
原物料
/能源
美國經濟擴張、政府加大基礎建設帶動基本金屬和建材需求，受惠於需求強勁、地緣政治造成物流
運輸受阻而支撐原物料價格表現。政府去監管政策，放寬傳統頁岩油氣、管線基礎設施環保管制。

非必須
消費
美國消費具韌性、就業市場穩健，民眾可支配所得增加，消費非必要高單價商品或娛樂餐飲帶動企
業營運。高所得族群支出不受景氣波動，企業獲利具抗震力，聚焦穩定現金流企業。
債券
市場
投等債 發債企業多為體質良好的大型企業，獲利與財務槓桿維持在健康水準，違約風險低。目前殖利率處
於5.5%相對高檔區間，提供穩固的票息保護墊。建議迴避中長天期資產，降低利率波動敏感度。
非投等債 提供相對投資級債更高的殖利率與票息，以及相對較短存續期間，為資產配置帶來實質且穩定現金
流，同時對市場利率變動敏感度較低，價格波動相對穩健。擇優佈局基本面穩健企業保持低違約率。
匯率
市場 美元 美國就業數據強勁，長天期公債殖利率飆升、融資成本惡化，不利吸引國際資金流入，加上地緣政
治與避險情緒猶存，支撐美國公債殖利率維持相對高檔，Fedwatch顯示年內具升息機會。

市場
歐洲 德國推出公共投資與軍事基建計畫，歐洲在AI基礎設施硬體與供應鏈以及資料中心電氣化元件具壟
斷性或高成長優勢。歐洲鄰近中東、俄烏地緣政治衝突核心，經濟易受國際大宗商品價格波動干擾。
日本 薪資溫和成長，內需動能回溫。政策扶植半導體、自動化設備等科技供應鏈。日銀推進利率正常化，
改善大型銀行利差收益。東證持續要求企業提升資本效率與股東回報，激發庫藏股買回與分紅熱潮。
健護醫療 高齡化帶動肥胖、癌症、心血管疾病等慢性病處方需求，剛性支出塑造抗週期與防禦特性。減肥藥
熱潮、AI醫療創新挹注產業盈餘爆發。醫療服務機構和醫院運營商受人員短缺和成本墊高侵蝕淨利。
地產 AI與雲端運算的硬體需求使科技巨頭大量投資AI基礎設施。人口結構改變使高齡住宅與中南部長租
別墅市場出租率居高不下。遠距與混合辦公趨勢已常態化，導致非頂級辦公大樓空置率難以改善。
工業 供應鏈回流帶動了自動化設備與工業檢測的需求。AI電力需求帶動老舊電網全面升級，挹注電器設
備電機、冷卻系統及能源管理方案供應商長期訂單。國防預算提升與航太需求復甦挹注訂單需求。

公用事業 電網、設備與融資成本上升影響資本支出回報，高利率環境下估值易被壓縮。受惠於數據中心帶動
的強勁電力需求，公用事業類股已從傳統的防禦型資產轉型為兼具穩健配息與成長潛力標的。
金融 民生用電與基礎設施需求具備剛性，提供穩定的殖利率與防禦特性。AI數據中心電力需求促使美國
電力公司規畫至2030年投入1.4兆美元基礎設施資本支出，亦可能帶來龐大的債務與融資壓力。

市場
公債 美國聯邦債務突破40兆美元，開支難以削減下政府須擴大發債，聯準會高利率維持時間比預期更長。
新興市場 殖利率具吸引力，配置主權信用品質改善部位可分散投組風險，迴避低信評、具政治/匯率風險國家。

Document 659: 本轮AI牛市两大“网红”合并！SemiAnalysis收购Citrini

Source PDF: all report pdf/本轮AI牛市两大“网红”合并！SemiAnalysis收购Citrini.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

此次收购将 SemiAnalysis 在芯⽚供应链与 AI 硬件领域的深度技术研究，与 Citrini 在公开市
场投资分析⽅⾯的影响⼒相结合。两者合并，被市场视为⼀个明确信号：将 AI 硬件建设与
公开市场股票表现相连接的综合性研究，正成为稀缺且⾼价值的资产。
摘要：
半导体与 AI 基础设施研究领域的两⼤独⽴研究机构正式⾛到⼀起。
据彭博社报道，SemiAnalysis 已收购主题投资研究机构 Citrini Research，双⽅创始⼈ Dylan
Patel 与 James van Geelen 于周五（ 9 ⽉ 11 ⽇）向彭博社确认了这⼀交易。此次收购将
SemiAnalysis 在芯⽚供应链与 AI 硬件领域的深度技术研究，与 Citrini 在公开市场投资分析⽅⾯的
影响⼒相结合，标志着独⽴研究⾏业在 AI 浪潮下的⼀次重要整合。交易价格未予披露。
据彭博报道， van Geelen 将暂时留任 Citrini Research ⾸席执⾏官， Citrini 将保持⼀定程度的运

营独⽴性，⽽⾮完全并⼊ SemiAnalysis 品牌。与此同时，报道指出，知情⼈⼠透露， van
Geelen 还计划另⾏发起⼀只新基⾦，但他本⼈拒绝就此置评。
这两家机构均是本轮 AI ⽜市中市场⾼度关注的独⽴研究声⾳。Citrini 今年早些时候发布的⼀篇报
告曾引发市场震荡，重创软件股； SemiAnalysis 则⻓期是芯⽚⾏业⾼管与机构投资者的必读参
考。两者合并，被市场视为⼀个明确信号：将 AI 硬件建设与公开市场股票表现相连接的综合性
研究，正成为稀缺且⾼价值的资产。
Citrini ：靠⼀篇报告震动市场的 " ⽹红 " 研究机构
Citrini Research 成⽴于 2023 年，是⼀家相对年轻的主题投资研究机构，总部位于纽约。尽管成
⽴时间不⻓，该机构已在 Substack 平台积累了约26 万订阅⽤⼾，是⾦融领域规模最⼤的
Substack 订阅业务之⼀。 Citrini 截⾄ 2025 年 12 ⽉已累计融资约505 万美元。
令 Citrini 真正出圈的，是 van Geelen 今年早些时候发布的⼀篇题为《 2028 年全球智能危机》的

报告。报告描绘了⼀个 AI 快速消灭⽩领⼯作、经济体系来不及消化冲击的未来图景，发布后迅速
在市场引发轩然⼤波，软件股遭到集中抛售。这篇报告让 Citrini 从⼀家订阅制研究机构跃升为具
备市场移动能⼒的研究⼒量。
SemiAnalysis ：芯⽚圈的 " 必读 " 研究机构

链、定制芯⽚路线图及数据中⼼经济学等核⼼议题，已成为芯⽚⾏业⾼管与机构投资者的重要
参考来源。

他认为，研究⾏业正在经历深刻变⾰，新时代的研究应当由真正懂⾏的⼈来完成，并向更⼴泛
的受众开放。
"James 拥有⾦融领域规模最⼤的 Substack 业务，同时在此之外也有其他业务， "Patel 表⽰， "这
次合并是为了扩⼤我们的研究规模，在这个研究⺠主化的新时代成为最优秀的研究机构。 "

欧洲天然⽓市场⾯临 2022 年以来最严峻供应压⼒：库存充填率仅 68% ，远低于 85% 的历史
均值；沙特输油管道遭袭及中东地缘冲突扰动航运， LNG 到港量回落，补库受阻。 TTF 期
货⼀度⼤涨 5.3% ⾄ 83.67 欧元 / 兆⽡时，今年累计涨约两倍。分析师警告，今冬若遇寒潮或
供应冲击，价格恐剧烈波动。
摘要：
欧洲天然⽓市场正⾯临⾃ 2022 年俄乌冲突爆发初期以来最为严峻的供应压⼒。库存严重低于历
史均值，中东地缘冲突持续扰动关键航运通道，多重⻛险叠加之下，今冬⼀旦出现寒潮，欧洲
能源市场或将承受剧烈的价格冲击。
荷兰 TTF 基准天然⽓期货周⼀早盘⼀度⼤涨 5.3% ，触及每兆⽡时 83.67 欧元，创 2022 年 12 ⽉以
来新⾼。今年以来，该合约价格已累计上涨约两倍。此轮涨势的直接导⽕索，是沙特阿拉伯东
西向输油管道近⽇遭⽆⼈机袭击，相关泵站基础设施受损后被迫停运。
⽬前，欧洲储⽓设施充填率仅为 68% ，远低于该时节约 85% 的 15 年历史均值。与此同时，上周

流⼊西欧的液化天然⽓（ LNG ）到港量在 9 ⽉初短暂回升后再度回落，库存补充进程明显放缓。
分析⼈⼠警告，低库存将使欧洲今冬能源供需平衡更加脆弱，任何寒潮或供应冲击都可能引发
剧烈价格波动。
库存缺⼝显著，补库进程受阻
欧洲进⼊供暖季时的储⽓⽔平已⼤幅落后于历史常态。 68% 的充填率与 85% 的 15 年均值之间存
在约 17 个百分点的差距，意味着欧洲在应对冬季需求⾼峰时的缓冲空间明显收窄。
LNG 进⼝的波动进⼀步加剧了补库压⼒。据报道，流⼊西欧的 LNG 到港量上周再度⾛弱，此前 9
⽉初的短暂回升未能持续，库存回补速度随之放缓。ING Groep NV 分析师在研报中指出， “ 这⼀
延误使任何局势缓和的前景愈发遥不可及。 ”
地缘⻛险是当前欧洲天然⽓市场⾯临的另⼀重要变量。霍尔⽊兹海峡与曼德海峡两⼤全球能源
运输咽喉要道均处于紧张态势，胡塞武装持续在红海地区发动袭击，沙特东西向管道基础设施
亦遭受⽆⼈机打击。
上述局势直接影响 LNG 货轮的航运路线与到港时效，令欧洲在冬季需求旺季前的补库窗⼝期进

⼀步压缩。
